Define

T^{*}:=\inf\{|t|:\tilde{N}(w(t))\geq 4\delta^{2}\}.

Then
\tilde{N}(w(t))\leq 4\delta^{2}
for all
|t|\leq T^{*}
. In particular, we have

\tilde{N}(w(t))=4\delta^{2},\quad{\rm for}\,\,t=T^{*}\,{\rm or}-T^{*}.

Without loss of generality, we assume

\tilde{N}(w(T^{*}))=4\delta^{2}.

(4.78)

Now we are in a position to show
T^{*}>\delta^{-\mathcal{M}/2}
. For each
(x,y,z,\bar{z})\in D(\varrho,2\delta,2\delta)
, from the definition of Poisson bracket under the symplectic structure (
2.2
) and (
4.77
), we have

\displaystyle|\{T,\tilde{N}\}(z,\bar{z})|\leq

\displaystyle(\|{\bf i}\kappa\partial_{z}T\|_{p-1}+\|-{\bf i}\kappa\partial_{\bar{z}}T\|_{p-1})\|z\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta||X_{T}||_{p-1,D(\varrho,2\delta,2\delta)}\|z\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\delta^{\mathcal{M}+\frac{5}{2}}.

(4.79)

By (
4.77
), one can obtain

\displaystyle|\{Q,\tilde{N}\}(x,y,z,\bar{z}))|\leq

\displaystyle(\|{\bf i}\kappa\partial_{z}T\|_{p/2}+\|-{\bf i}\kappa\partial_{\bar{z}}T\|_{p/2})\|z\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta||X_{T}||_{p/2,D(\varrho,2\delta,2\delta)}\|z\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\delta^{\mathcal{M}+\frac{5}{2}}.

(4.80)

Thus, one gets

\begin{split}|\tilde{N}(w(t))-\tilde{N}(w(0))|=&|\int_{0}^{t}\{H^{**},\tilde{N}\}(w(s))ds|\\
=&|\int_{0}^{t}\{N+Z+B+Q+T,\tilde{N}\}(w(s))ds|\\
\leq&\int_{0}^{t}|\{Q+T,\tilde{N}\}(w(s))|ds\\
&(N,\ Z\ {\rm and}\ B\ {\rm depend}\ {\rm on}\ |z|^{2})\\
\leq&32\delta^{\mathcal{M}+\frac{5}{2}}|t|\\
&({\rm by}\ \eqref{*}\ {\rm and}\ \eqref{**})\\
\leq&32\delta^{\mathcal{M}+\frac{5}{2}}T^{*}.\end{split}

(4.81)

Proof by contradiction: If
T^{*}<\delta^{-\mathcal{M}/2}
, then

\displaystyle 4\delta^{2}=

\displaystyle|\tilde{N}(w(T))|\leq|\tilde{N}(w(0))|+|\tilde{N}(w(t))-\tilde{N}(w(0))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{2}+32\delta^{(\mathcal{M}+{5})/{2}}\delta^{-\mathcal{M}/2}<2\delta^{2},

which is impossible.

In the following, we will estimate
||y(t)||
. For
1\leq j\leq n
, we let

\tilde{Y}_{j}(y):=y_{j}.

By the initial data, we have

\tilde{Y}(w(0))\leq 4\delta^{2}.

Define

T_{j}:=\inf\{|t|:\tilde{Y}_{j}(w(t))\geq 8\delta^{2}\}.

Then
\tilde{Y}_{j}(w(t))\leq 8\delta^{2}
for all
|t|\leq T_{j}
. In particular, we have

\tilde{Y}_{j}(w(t))=8\delta^{2},\quad{\rm for}\,\,|t|=T_{j}\,{\rm or}-T_{j}.

Without loss of generality, we assume

\tilde{Y}_{j}(w(T_{j}))=8\delta^{2}.

(4.82)

Now we are in a position to show
T_{j}>\delta^{-\mathcal{M}/4}
.

From (
4.81
), we have
|\sum_{j}|j|^{2p/2}(|z_{j}(t)|^{2}-|z_{j}(0)|^{2})|\leq|t|\delta^{(\mathcal{M}+{5})/{2}}
. It follows that

\left|\left\{\sum_{j\geq 1}\frac{(\breve{\Omega}_{j}-[\breve{\Omega}_{j}])(x;\xi)}{j}(|z_{j}|^{2}-|z_{j}(0)|^{2}),\tilde{Y}_{j}\right\}\right|\leq|t|\delta^{(\mathcal{M}+{5})/{2}},

(4.83)

and by (
4.76
) and Cauchy’s estimate

\displaystyle|\{B,\tilde{Y}_{j}\}(x,y,z,\bar{z})|\leq||\partial_{x}B||

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle||\sum_{|\beta|\geq 1}\partial_{x}B^{\beta}(x,y;\xi)(|z|^{2}-|z(0)|^{2})^{\beta}||

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varrho}\delta^{2-}\sum_{|\beta|\geq 1}(|t|\delta^{(\mathcal{M}+{5})/{2}})^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\delta^{(\mathcal{M}+{5})/{2}}|t|,

(4.84)

for some
|t|\leq T^{*}
.
In addition, from (
4.74
) and (
4.75
), we have

\displaystyle|\{Z,\tilde{Y}_{j}\}(x,y,z,\bar{z})|\leq||\partial_{x}Z||

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle||\sum_{|\alpha|\geq\mathcal{M}+3}\partial_{x}Z^{\alpha}(x;\xi)y^{\alpha}||

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\geq\mathcal{M}+3}\frac{1}{\varrho}\delta^{2-}\cdot\delta^{2\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\delta^{\mathcal{M}+\frac{5}{2}}.

(4.85)

By (
4.77
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\{T,\tilde{Y}_{j}\}(x,y,z,\bar{z})|\leq||\partial_{x}T||\leq 16\delta^{\mathcal{M}+\frac{5}{2}},

(4.86)

and

\displaystyle|\{Q,\tilde{Y}_{j}\}(x,y,z,\bar{z})|\leq||\partial_{x}Q||\leq 16\delta^{\mathcal{M}+\frac{5}{2}}.

(4.87)

Thus, by (
4.83
), (
4.84
), (
4.86
) and (
4.87
), one gets

\displaystyle|\tilde{Y}_{j}(w(t))-\tilde{Y}_{j}(w(0))|=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\{H^{**},\tilde{Y}_{j}\}(w(s))ds\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\{N+Z+B+Q+T,\tilde{Y}_{j}\}(w(s))ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 32\delta^{(\mathcal{M}+{5})/{2}}T_{j}^{2}.

Proof by contradiction: If
T_{j}<\delta^{-\mathcal{M}/4}
, then

\displaystyle 8\delta^{2}=

\displaystyle|\tilde{N}(w(T))|\leq|\tilde{N}(w(0))|+|\tilde{N}(w(t))-\tilde{N}(w(0))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\delta^{2}+64\delta^{(\mathcal{M}+{5})/{2}}\delta^{-\mathcal{M}/2}<5\delta^{2},

which is impossible.

As a consequence, for all
|t|\leq\min\{T_{*},T_{j}(1\leq j\leq n)\}=\delta^{-\mathcal{M}/4}
, from (
4.78
) and (
4.82
) we have

\frac{1}{4\delta}||w_{y}(t)||+||w_{z}(t)||_{p/2}+||w_{\bar{z}}(t)||_{p/2}\leq 2\delta.

That is,

d_{p/2}(w(t),\mathcal{T}_{0})\leq 2\delta.

(4.88)

Based on the partial normal form (
4.1
) constructed in Theorem
4.1
, for each
\xi\in\mathcal{T}
of the original Hamiltonian
H
can be defined by
\mathcal{T}=(\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**})^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{0}
. Assume
\tilde{w}(t)
is a solution to the original Hamiltonian vector field
X_{H}
with the initial datum
\tilde{w}(0)=(\tilde{w}_{x}(0),\tilde{w}_{y}(0),\tilde{w}_{z}(0),\tilde{w}_{\bar{z}}(0))
satisfying

d_{p}(\tilde{w}(0),\mathcal{T})\leq\delta.

Then there exists
\tilde{w}^{*}\in\mathcal{T}
such that

d_{p}(\tilde{w}(0),\tilde{w}^{*})\leq\frac{9}{8}\delta.

Hence,

\displaystyle d_{p}(\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**}\circ\tilde{w}(0),\mathcal{T}_{0})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d_{p}(\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**}\circ\tilde{w}(0),\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**}\circ\tilde{w}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d_{p}(\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**}\circ\tilde{w}(0),\tilde{w}(0))+d_{p}(\tilde{w}(0),\tilde{w}^{*})+d_{p}(\tilde{w}^{*},\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**}\circ\tilde{w}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\delta||\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**}\circ\tilde{w}(0)-\tilde{w}(0)||_{p,D(\varrho,2\rho,4\rho)}+\frac{9}{8}\delta

\displaystyle+4\delta||\tilde{w}^{*},\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**}\circ\tilde{w}^{*}||_{p,D(\varrho,2\rho,4\rho)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{3}\delta,

where the last inequality comes from (
2.7
) and (
4.4
).
Therefore, according to the estimate (
4.88
), we have that, for
w(t)=\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**}\circ\tilde{w}(t)
,

d_{p/2}(\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**}\circ\tilde{w}(t),\mathcal{T}_{0})\leq\frac{5}{3}\delta,\quad{\rm for}\ \ {\rm all}\ \ |t|<\delta^{-\mathcal{M}/4}.

Moreover, we have

d_{p/2}(\tilde{w}(t),(\Psi\circ\Phi\circ\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{**})^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{0})\leq 2\delta,\quad{\rm for}\ \ {\rm all}\ \ |t|<\delta^{-\mathcal{M}/4}.

4.8
Proof of main Theorem
1.1

By subsection
2.1.1
, we obtain a Hamiltonian
H(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)
of the following form

H(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=N(y,z,\bar{z};\xi)+P(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi),

where

N(y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}(\xi)y_{i}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\frac{\Omega_{j}(\xi)}{j}z_{j}\bar{z}_{j},

with the tangent frequency

\omega(\xi)=(\omega_{1}(\xi),\cdots,\omega_{n}(\xi))=\left(j_{1}^{2}+\xi_{j_{1}},\cdots,j_{n}^{2}+\xi_{j_{n}}\right),

(4.89)

and the normal frequency
\left(\frac{\Omega_{j}(\xi)}{j}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}
where

(\Omega_{j}(\xi))_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}=\left(j^{2}+\xi_{j}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}.

(4.90)

The perturbation becomes
P(x,y,z,\bar{z})
which is independent of
\xi
.

In view of (
4.89
) and (
4.90
), it is easy to verify the
Assumption A, B
and
C
in Theorem
2.6
hold. Moreover, the perturbation
P(x,y,z,\bar{z})
satisfies
|||P|||_{D(s_{0},r_{0},r_{0})\times\Pi}<\epsilon
. Hence, all assumptions in Theorem
2.6
hold. Therefore, there exists a subset
\Pi_{\eta}\subset\Pi
with the estimate

{\rm Meas}\,\Pi_{\eta}\geq{\rm Meas}\,\Pi(1-O(\eta)).

For each
\xi\in\Pi_{\eta}
, there is symplectic transformation

\Psi:D(s_{0}/2,r_{0}/2,r_{0}/2)\rightarrow D(s_{0},r_{0},r_{0}),

such that

\breve{H}(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\breve{N}(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)+\breve{P}(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi),

where

\breve{N}(y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\breve{\omega}_{j}(\xi)y_{j}+\sum_{j\geq 1}\breve{\Omega}_{j}(x;\xi)z_{j}\bar{z}_{j}

=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\breve{\omega}_{j}(\xi)y_{j}+\sum_{|j|\leq\mathcal{N}}\breve{\Omega}_{j}(\xi)z_{j}\bar{z}_{j}+\sum_{|j|\geq\mathcal{N}+1}\breve{\Omega}_{j}(x;\xi)z_{j}\bar{z}_{j},

and

\breve{P}(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\beta,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{N}},\\
2|\alpha|+|\beta+\gamma|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\breve{P}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(x;\xi)y^{\alpha}z^{\beta}\bar{z}^{\gamma}.

Based on Theorem
2.8
, there is a subset
{\Pi}_{\acute{\eta}}
such that for each
\xi\in{\Pi}_{\acute{\eta}}
equation (
1.11
) possess a KAM tori
\mathcal{T}_{\xi}
in
H_{0}^{p}([0,\pi])
. In addition, for any solution
u(t,x)
of equation (
1.11
) with the initial datum satisfying

d_{H_{0}^{p}([0,\pi])}(u(0,x),\mathcal{T}_{\xi})\leq\delta,

then

d_{H_{0}^{p/2}([0,\pi])}(u(t,x),\mathcal{T}_{\xi})\leq 2\delta,\quad{\rm for}\,\,{\rm all}\,\,|t|\leq\delta^{-\mathcal{M}/4}.

5
Some technical lemmata

Lemma 5.1
.

Consider a function
W(x;\xi)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}W(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}
defined on the domain
D(s)\times\Pi
, which is analytic in
(x)\in D(s)
and
C^{1}
in
\xi\in\Pi
, then the following generalized Cauchy estimate holds

\|\partial_{x}W\|_{D(s-\sigma,r)\times\Pi}\leq\frac{1}{e\sigma}\|W\|_{D(s,r)\times\Pi},

where
0<\sigma<s
.

This proof of this Lemma can be found in Lemma 7.3 in
[
16
]
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Consider two functions

\begin{split}U(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)&=\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}(x;\xi)y^{\kappa}z^{\alpha}\bar{z}^{\beta}\in\mathcal{H}\\
&=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{n},\kappa\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\kappa}z^{\alpha}\bar{z}^{\beta},\end{split}

and

V(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\tau|+|\mu|+|\gamma|\leq 2\end{subarray}}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}(x;\xi)y^{\tau}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{H}_{*}

=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|k|\leq K,\tau\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\tau|+|\mu|+|\gamma|\leq 2\end{subarray}}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\tau}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma},

where the functions
U,V
defined on the domain
D(s,r,r)\times\Pi
are analytic in
(x,y,z,\bar{z})\in D(s,r,r)
and
C^{1}
in
\xi\in\Pi
. Then we have that
\{U,V\}
belongs to
\mathcal{H}
with the following estimate

|||\{U,V\}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}\leq C\max\Big\{\frac{K^{n}}{r\sigma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{r^{2}\sigma}\Big\}|||U|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}|||V|||^{*}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}.

Proof.

We consider the functions

U(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}(x;\xi)y^{\kappa}z^{\alpha}\bar{z}^{\beta}\in\mathcal{H}

=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{n},2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\kappa}z^{\alpha}\bar{z}^{\beta},

and

V(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=2}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}(x;\xi)y^{\tau}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{H}_{*},

=\sum_{|k|\leq K,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=2}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\tau}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}.

By the definition of Possion bracket, we have

\{U,V\}=\sum_{2|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}+k^{\prime}|=h}\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}y^{q^{\prime}}z^{l^{\prime}}\bar{z}^{k^{\prime}},

where, for
2|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}+k^{\prime}|=h
,

\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}=\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{1}+\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{2},

with

\displaystyle\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa+\tau-e_{j}=q^{\prime},\alpha+\mu=l^{\prime},\beta+\gamma=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=2\end{subarray}}\tau_{j}\partial_{x_{j}}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}

(5.1)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa-e_{j}+\tau=q^{\prime},\alpha+\mu=l^{\prime},\beta+\gamma=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=2\end{subarray}}\kappa_{j}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\partial_{x_{j}}V^{\tau\mu\gamma},

(5.2)

and

\displaystyle\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{2}=

\displaystyle{\bf i}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau+\kappa=q^{\prime},(\alpha-e_{j})+\mu=l^{\prime},\beta+(\gamma-e_{j})=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=2\end{subarray}}j\alpha_{j}\gamma_{j}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau+\kappa=q^{\prime},\alpha+(\mu-e_{j})=l^{\prime},(\beta-e_{j})+\gamma=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=2\end{subarray}}j\beta_{j}\mu_{j}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\Bigg).

(5.3)

Consider the term
\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{2}
. In what follows, we will show there are at most

2(2K)^{n}h

(5.4)

terms at the right hand of (
5.3
).

Consider the nonzero components of vector
q^{\prime},k^{\prime}
and
l^{\prime}
. Taking multiplicity into account, we have that
(q^{\prime},l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
has
|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+|k^{\prime}|
non zero components whose values are
1
. We denote them by

\displaystyle q_{j_{1}},\cdots,q_{j_{|q^{\prime}|}},l_{j_{|q^{\prime}|+1}},\cdots,l_{j_{|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|}},k_{j_{|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+1}},\cdots,k_{j_{|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+|k^{\prime}|}}.

If
(\tau,\mu,\gamma-e_{j})
is fixed, then
(\kappa,\alpha-e_{j},\beta)
can be determined by

\kappa+\tau=q^{\prime},\quad(\alpha-e_{j})+\mu=l^{\prime},\quad\beta+(\gamma-e_{j})=k^{\prime},

(5.5)

or

\kappa+\tau=q^{\prime},\quad\alpha+(\mu-e_{j})=l^{\prime},\quad(\beta-e_{j})+\gamma=k^{\prime}.

(5.6)

Therefore we choose
|\tau|+|\mu|+|\gamma|-1
positions from
|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+|k^{\prime}|
positions. Actually, there are
\left(\begin{array}[]{l}q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+|k^{\prime}|\\
|\tau|+|\mu|+|\gamma|-1\end{array}\right)
choices.
If
(\tau,\mu,\gamma-e_{j})
is fixed, the last position
j
i.e.,
e_{j}
will have
(2K)^{n}
choices, according to the momentum conservation:

-\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}j_{i}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}j(\mu_{j}-\gamma_{j})=0.

In conclusion, the number of pairs
(\tau,\mu,\gamma)
and
(\kappa,\alpha,\beta)
satisfying (
5.5
) and (
5.6
) equals

\displaystyle 2(2K)^{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+|k^{\prime}|\\
|\tau|+|\mu|+|\gamma|-1\end{array}\right)\leq

\displaystyle 2(2K)^{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+|k^{\prime}|+|\tau|\\
2|\tau|+|\mu|+|\gamma|-1\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(2K)^{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+|k^{\prime}|+|\tau|\\
1\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(2K)^{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}h\\
1\end{array}\right)=2(2K)^{n}h,

where we use
\left(\begin{array}[]{l}a\\
b\end{array}\right)\leq\left(\begin{array}[]{l}a+k\\
b+k\end{array}\right)
and
\left(\begin{array}[]{l}a\\
b\end{array}\right)\leq\left(\begin{array}[]{l}a+l\\
b\end{array}\right)
.

Similarly, one can prove there is at most

nh

(5.15)

terms at the right hand of (
5.2
). In addition, by the special form of
V
, it is easy to find that there are
n
terms in (
5.1
). Therefore, we have

\|\eqref{lpkp2}\|\leq n\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|\partial_{x}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|

\leq\frac{n}{e\sigma}\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|

and

\|\eqref{lpkp}\|\leq nh\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|\partial_{x}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|

\leq nhK\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|,

since
V
is a truncated Fourier series of
K
-th order.

From (
5
) and (
5.15
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\|_{D(s-\sigma)\times\Pi}&\leq\max\Big\{\big(2(2K)^{n}+nK\big)h,\frac{1}{e\sigma}n\Big\}\\
&\cdot\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}M_{\mu\gamma}\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}.\end{split}

Then, we have

\displaystyle(r-\sigma^{\prime})^{h}\|\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\|_{D(s-\sigma)\times\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\max\Big\{K^{n}h,\frac{1}{\sigma}\Big\}\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}\cdot M_{\mu\gamma}\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}(r-\sigma^{\prime})^{h}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\max\Big\{\frac{K^{n}}{\sigma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{r\sigma}\Big\}\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}\cdot M_{\mu\gamma}\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}r^{h+1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\max\Big\{\frac{K^{n}}{r\sigma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{r^{2}\sigma}\Big\}\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}r^{h}\cdot M_{\mu\gamma}\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}r^{2},

where the constant
C
only depends on
n
.
The second inequality comes from
h(r-\sigma^{\prime})^{h-1}\sigma^{\prime}\leq r^{h}
.
It follows that:

|||\{U,V\}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}\leq C\max\Big\{\frac{K^{n}}{r\sigma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{r^{2}\sigma}\Big\}|||U|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}|||V|||^{*}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}.

(5.16)

Similarly, we can prove that (
5.16
) still holds after replacing
U,V
by

U(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}(x;\xi)y^{\kappa}z^{\alpha}\bar{z}^{\beta}\in\mathcal{H},

and

V(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=g}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}(x;\xi)y^{\tau}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{H}_{*},\quad g=0,1.

Finally, we consider more general functions

U(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{h\geq 0}U_{h}(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi),

V(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{g\geq 0}U_{g}(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi),

where

U_{h}(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=h\end{subarray}}U^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(x;\xi)y^{\alpha}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma},

V_{g}(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=g\end{subarray}}V^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(x;\xi)y^{\alpha}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}.

By a direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle|||\{U,V\}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|||\{\sum_{h\geq 0}U_{h},\sum_{g\geq 0}V_{g}\}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{h,g\geq 0}|||\{U_{h},V_{g}\}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{h,g\geq 0}CK^{n}\frac{1}{r\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}|||U_{h}|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}|||V_{g}|||^{*}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\max\Big\{\frac{K^{n}}{r\sigma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{r^{2}\sigma}\Big\}\left(\sum_{h\geq 0}|||U_{h}|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}\right)\left(\sum_{g\geq 0}|||V_{g}|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\max\Big\{\frac{K^{n}}{r\sigma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{r^{2}\sigma}\Big\}|||U|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}|||V|||^{*}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}.

Thus, we finish the proof of this Lemma.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Consider two Hamiltonians
U(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)\in\mathcal{H}
and
V(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)\in\mathcal{H}^{*}
defined on the domain
D(s,r,r)\times\Pi
for some
0<s,r\leq 1
. In addition, we assume
U,V
satisfies the condition in Lemma
5.2
. Given
0<\sigma<s,0<\sigma^{\prime}<r
. Suppose

|||V|||^{*}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}\leq\frac{1}{2A},

where

A=C_{0}e\max\Big\{\frac{K^{n}}{r\sigma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{r^{2}\sigma}\Big\},

and
C_{0}
is the constant given in Lemma
5.2
. Then for each
|t|\leq 1
, we have

|||U\circ X_{V}^{t}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}\leq 2|||U|||_{{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}.

Proof.

Let

W^{(j)}=\{\cdots\{U,V\},V\},\underbrace{V\}\cdots\},V}_{j-fold}\}.

For
j\geq 1
, let
\sigma_{j}=\frac{\sigma}{j}
and
\sigma_{j}^{\prime}=\frac{\sigma_{j}^{\prime}}{j}
. Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle|||W^{(j)}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|||W^{(j)}|||_{D(s-j\sigma_{j},r-j\sigma_{j}^{\prime},r-j\sigma_{j}^{\prime})\times\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(C_{0}\max\Big\{\frac{K^{n}}{r\sigma_{j}^{\prime}},\frac{1}{r^{2}\sigma_{j}}\Big\}\right)^{j}\left(|||U|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}\right)\left(|||V|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}\right)^{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle j^{j}\left(C_{0}\max\Big\{\frac{K^{n}}{r\sigma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{r^{2}\sigma}\Big\}\right)^{j}\left(|||U|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}\right)\left(|||V|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}\right)^{j}.

Using the inequality

j^{j}\leq j!e^{j},

one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{1}{j!}|||W^{(j)}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{j}\left(C_{0}\max\Big\{\frac{K^{n}}{r\sigma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{r^{2}\sigma}\Big\}\right)^{j}\left(|||U|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}\right)\left(|||V|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}\right)^{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(|||U|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}\right)\left(A|||V|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}\right)^{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{j}}|||U|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}.

Expanding the Hamiltonian
U\circ X_{V}^{t}
into Taylor series about
t
at
t=0
, we get

\displaystyle|||U\circ X_{V}^{t}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|||\sum_{j\geq 0}\frac{t^{j}}{j!}W^{(j)}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 0}\frac{1}{j!}|||W^{(j)}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 0}\frac{1}{2^{j}}|||U|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2|||U|||_{{}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}}.

∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Consider two functions

U(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|k|\in\mathbb{Z}^{n},\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h\end{subarray}}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\kappa}z^{\alpha}\bar{z}^{\beta},

and

V(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|k|\leq K,\tau\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\tau|+|\mu|+|\gamma|=g\end{subarray}}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\tau}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma},

where the functions
U,V
defined on the domain
D(s,r,r)\times\Pi
are analytic in
(x,y,z,\bar{z})\in D(s,r,r)
and
C^{1}
in
\xi\in\Pi
. We assume
V
has at most degree
2
in
(z_{j},\bar{z}_{j})_{|j|>N}
.
Then we have that
\{U,V\}
belongs to
\mathcal{H}
with the following estimate

|||\{U,V\}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}\leq C\frac{K^{n+1}(2N+n)^{g-2}}{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}r}|||U|||_{D(s-\sigma,r,r)\times\Pi}|||V|||^{*}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}.

Remark 5.5
.

The proof of this lemma follows from the proof of Lemma 4.6 of
[
40
]
.

Proof.

By the definition of Possion bracket, we have

\{U,V\}=\sum_{2|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}+k^{\prime}|=h+g-2}\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}y^{q^{\prime}}z^{l^{\prime}}\bar{z}^{k^{\prime}},

where, for
2|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}+k^{\prime}|=h+g-2
,

\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}=\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{1}+\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{2},

with

\displaystyle\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa+\tau-e_{j}=q^{\prime},\alpha+\mu=l^{\prime},\beta+\gamma=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=g\end{subarray}}\tau_{j}\partial_{x_{j}}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}

(5.17)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa-e_{j}+\tau=q^{\prime},\alpha+\mu=l^{\prime},\beta+\gamma=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=g\end{subarray}}\kappa_{j}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\partial_{x_{j}}V^{\tau\mu\gamma},

(5.18)

and

\displaystyle\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{2}=

\displaystyle{\bf i}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau+\kappa=q^{\prime},(\alpha-e_{j})+\mu=l^{\prime},\beta+(\gamma-e_{j})=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=g\end{subarray}}j\alpha_{j}\gamma_{j}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau+\kappa=q^{\prime},\alpha+(\mu-e_{j})=l^{\prime},(\beta-e_{j})+\gamma=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=g\end{subarray}}j\beta_{j}\mu_{j}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\Bigg).

(5.19)

In addition, we write

\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{2}:=\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{21}+\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{22},

with

\displaystyle\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{21}=

\displaystyle{\bf i}\sum_{0<|j|\leq N}\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau+\kappa=q^{\prime},(\alpha-e_{j})+\mu=l^{\prime},\beta+(\gamma-e_{j})=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=g\end{subarray}}j\alpha_{j}\gamma_{j}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau+\kappa=q^{\prime},\alpha+(\mu-e_{j})=l^{\prime},(\beta-e_{j})+\gamma=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=g\end{subarray}}j\beta_{j}\mu_{j}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\Bigg),

\displaystyle\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{22}=

\displaystyle{\bf i}\sum_{|j|>N}\Bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau+\kappa=q^{\prime},(\alpha-e_{j})+\mu=l^{\prime},\beta+(\gamma-e_{j})=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=g\end{subarray}}j\alpha_{j}\gamma_{j}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau+\kappa=q^{\prime},\alpha+(\mu-e_{j})=l^{\prime},(\beta-e_{j})+\gamma=k^{\prime}\\
2|\kappa|+|\alpha+\beta|=h,2|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|=g\end{subarray}}j\beta_{j}\mu_{j}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\Bigg).

Consider the nonzero components of vector
q^{\prime},k^{\prime}
and
l^{\prime}
. Taking multiplicity into account, we have that
(q^{\prime},l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
has
|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+|k^{\prime}|
non zero components whose values are
1
. We denote them by

\displaystyle q_{j_{1}},\cdots,q_{j_{|q^{\prime}|}},l_{j_{|q^{\prime}|+1}},\cdots,l_{j_{|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|}},k_{j_{|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+1}},\cdots,k_{j_{|q^{\prime}|+|l^{\prime}|+|k^{\prime}|}}.

The estimates of
\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{21}
can be obtained as follows.

Since
V
has at most degree
2
in
(z_{j},\bar{z}_{j})_{|j|>N}
, there are at least
|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|-3
components of
(q^{\prime},l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
coming from
(\tau,\mu,\gamma)
with indexes bounded by
N
or coming from
1,\cdots,n
. Therefore, the choice of
|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|-3
components of
(q^{\prime},l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
is less than
(2N+n)^{|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|-3}(\leq(2N+n)^{g-3})
. As for the remaining three components of
(\tau,\mu,\gamma)
, one position is
j
with
|j|\leq N
and the choice is
2N
. Another position among the other two can be selected from the rest of the nonzero components
(q^{\prime},l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
, the choice is
\left(\begin{array}[]{l}1\\
|\kappa|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\end{array}\right)=|\kappa|+|\alpha|+|\beta|(\leq h)
. The last one may be determined by the momentum conservation of
V
. Thus, we conclude that

\displaystyle\|\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{21}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}\leq

\displaystyle(2K)^{n}h(2N+n)^{g-3}2N

\displaystyle\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}\cdot M_{\mu\gamma}\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}.

The estimate of
\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{22}
can be obtained as follows.

Since
V
has degree at most
2
in
(z_{j},\bar{z}_{j})_{|j|>N}
, there are at least
|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|-2
components of
(q^{\prime},l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
coming from
(\tau,\mu,\gamma)
with indexes bounded by
N
or coming from
1,\cdots,n
and the choice of
|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|-2
components of
(q^{\prime},l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
is less than
(2N+n)^{|\tau|+|\mu+\gamma|-2}
. One position of the last two component can be selected from the rest of the nonzero components
(q^{\prime},l^{\prime},k^{\prime})
, the choice is
\left(\begin{array}[]{l}1\\
|\kappa|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\end{array}\right)=|\kappa|+|\alpha|+|\beta|
. The last one position may be determined by the momentum conservation of
V
. Thus, we conclude that

\displaystyle\|\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{21}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}\leq

\displaystyle(2K)^{n}h(2N+n)^{g-2}

\displaystyle\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}\cdot M_{\mu\gamma}\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}.

Similarly, one can prove

\displaystyle\|\{U,V\}^{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}k^{\prime}}_{1}\|_{D(s-\sigma)\times\Pi}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2K)^{n}h(2N+n)^{g-2}\Big(\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|\partial_{x}U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|_{D(s-\sigma)\times\Pi}\cdot\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|_{D(s-\sigma)\times\Pi}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|_{D(s-\sigma)\times\Pi}\cdot\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|\partial_{x}V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|_{D(s-\sigma)\times\Pi}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2K)^{n}h(2N+n)^{g-2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma}\sup_{\kappa,\alpha,\beta}\|U^{\kappa\alpha\beta}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}\cdot\sup_{\tau,\mu,\gamma}\|V^{\tau\mu\gamma}\|_{D(s)\times\Pi}.

Thus, one can easily obtains

|||\{U,V\}|||_{D(s-\sigma,r-\sigma^{\prime},r-\sigma^{\prime})\times\Pi}\leq C\frac{K^{n+1}(2N+n)^{g-2}}{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}r}|||U|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}|||V|||^{*}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}.

∎

The following is the Tame property which is useful for the long time stability estimate of KAM tori.

Lemma 5.6
.

Suppose the function

W(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=h\end{subarray}}W^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(x;\xi)y^{\alpha}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}:D(s,r,r)\times\Pi\rightarrow\mathbb{R},

belongs to
\mathcal{H}
. Then, the following estimate hold

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}W\|_{D(s-\sigma,r,r)\times\Pi}\leq\frac{c_{1}^{h-2}}{e\sigma}|||W|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}r^{-h}\sum_{\alpha}|y|^{|\alpha|}\|z\|_{2}^{h-2|\alpha|},

(5.20)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}W\|_{D(s-\sigma,r,r)\times\Pi}\leq c_{1}^{h-2}h|||W|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}r^{-h}\sum_{\alpha}|y|^{|\alpha|-1}\|z\|_{2}^{h-2|\alpha|},

(5.21)

\begin{split}\|({\bf i}j\partial_{\bar{z}_{j}}W)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\|_{p-1},\|(-{\bf i}j\partial_{z_{j}}W)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\|_{p-1}&\leq c_{1}^{h-2}h^{p+2}\frac{C_{J_{n}}}{e}|||W|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}r^{-h},\\
&\cdot\sum_{\alpha}(\frac{1}{\sigma^{p-1}}|y|^{|\alpha|}\|z\|_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|-1}+\|z\|_{s}\|z\|_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|-2}),\end{split}

where
c_{1}=\sqrt{\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}j^{-2}}
and
C_{J_{n}}
is a constant depending on the index set
{J_{n}}
. In addition, the following estimate hold

\|X_{W}\|_{p-1,D(s-\sigma,r,r)\times\Pi}\leq c^{h-2}\frac{h^{p+2}}{\sigma^{p}}|||W|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}r^{-2},

where
c
is a constant .

Proof.

For
\partial_{x}W
, we have

\partial_{x}W(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{n},\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=h\end{subarray}}{\bf i}kW^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\alpha}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}.

Thus, we obtain

\|\partial_{x}W\|_{D(s-\sigma,r,r)\times\Pi}\leq\sum_{k,\alpha}|k|e^{|k|(s-\sigma)}\sup_{\mu,\gamma}|W^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(k;\xi)||y|^{\alpha}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|\\
M(k,\mu,\gamma)=0\end{subarray}}|z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}|.

By Hölder inequality, we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|\\
M(k,\mu,\gamma)=0\end{subarray}}|z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}|\leq\Bigg\|\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mu+\gamma|=h-2\alpha\\
j=M(k,\mu-e_{j},\gamma)\end{subarray}}|z^{\mu-e_{j}}z^{\gamma}|\Big)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\Bigg\|_{l^{2}}\cdot\|z\|_{l^{2}},

It follows from the Young inequality

\Big\|(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}a_{j\pm k}b_{k})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\Big\|_{l^{2}}\leq\|a\|_{l^{2}}\|b\|_{l^{1}}

that, for each fixed
k
, the following estimate holds

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|\\
M(k,\mu,\gamma)=0\end{subarray}}|z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}|\leq c_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|-2}\sum_{2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=h}\|z\|_{1}^{|\mu+\gamma|}.

Thus, we get

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}W\|_{D(s-\sigma,r,r)\times\Pi}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}^{h-2}}{e\sigma}\sup_{\alpha,\mu,\gamma}\|W^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(x;\xi)\|\sum_{2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=h}|y|^{\alpha}\|z\|_{1}^{|\mu+\gamma|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}^{h-2}}{e\sigma}|||W|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}r^{-h}\sum_{\alpha}|y|^{|\alpha|}\|z\|_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|},

(5.22)

Similarly, for
\partial_{y}W
, one has

\partial_{y}W(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{n},\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=h\end{subarray}}\alpha W^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\alpha-1}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}.

Therefore, one can prove

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}W\|_{D(s-\sigma,r,r)\times\Pi}\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}^{h-2}h|||W|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}r^{-h}\sum_{\alpha}|y|^{|\alpha|-1}\|z\|_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|}.

(5.23)

For
({\bf i}j\partial_{\bar{z}_{j}}W,-{\bf i}j\partial_{z_{j}}W)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}
, we have

\displaystyle({\bf i}j\partial_{\bar{z}_{j}}W,-{\bf i}j\partial_{z_{j}}W)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big({\bf i}j\gamma_{j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{n},\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=h\end{subarray}}W^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\alpha}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma-e_{j}},

\displaystyle-{\bf i}j\mu_{j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}^{n},\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=h\end{subarray}}W^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\alpha}z^{\mu-e_{j}}\bar{z}^{\gamma}\Big)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\|({\bf i}j\partial_{\bar{z}_{j}}W)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\|_{p-1}=\|(\partial_{\bar{z}_{j}}W)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\|_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\left(\sum_{k,2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=h}|\gamma_{j}||W^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(k;\xi)e^{{\bf i}\langle k,x\rangle}y^{\alpha}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma-e_{j}}|\cdot|j|^{p}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k,\alpha}\sup_{\mu,\gamma}|W^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(k;\xi)e^{|k|(s-\sigma)}|y|^{\alpha}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\left(\sum_{|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|}|\gamma_{j}||z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma-e_{j}}|\cdot|j|^{p}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.24)

Since
j=M(k,\mu-e_{j},\gamma)=\sum_{b}k_{b}j_{b}-\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{*},\beta\neq j}\beta\mu_{\beta}+\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\beta\gamma_{\beta}
, we have

|j|^{p}\leq h^{p}\big(C_{J_{n}}|k|^{p}+\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}|\beta|^{p}\mu_{\beta}+\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{*},\beta\neq j}|\beta|^{p}\gamma_{\beta}+(\gamma_{j}-1)|j|^{p}\big),

where
C_{J_{n}}
is a constant depending on the index set
J_{n}
.
Therefore, one gets

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\left(\sum_{|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|}|\gamma_{j}||z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma-e_{j}}|\cdot|j|^{p}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle h^{p}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\left(\sum_{|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|}|\gamma_{j}||z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma-e_{j}}|\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\cdot\Big(C_{J_{n}}|k|^{p}+\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}|\beta|^{p}\mu_{\beta}+\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{*},\beta\neq j}|\beta|^{p}\gamma_{\beta}+(\gamma_{j}-1)|j|^{p}\Big)\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle h^{p}C_{J_{n}}|k|^{p}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\left(\sum_{|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|}|\gamma_{j}||z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma-e_{j}}|\ \right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+h^{p}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\left(\sum_{|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|}|\gamma_{j}||z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma-e_{j}}|\cdot|t|^{p}\mu_{t}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+h^{p}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{Z}^{*},t\neq j}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\left(\sum_{|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|}|\gamma_{j}||z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma-e_{j}}|\cdot|t|^{p}\gamma_{t}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+h^{p}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\left(\sum_{|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|}|\gamma_{j}||z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma-e_{j}}|\cdot(\gamma_{j}-1)|j|^{p}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\left(\sum_{|\mu+\gamma|=h-2|\alpha|}|\gamma_{j}||z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma-e_{j}}|\cdot|j|^{p}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle h^{p+2}C_{J_{n}}|k|^{p}c_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|-1}\|z\|_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|-1}+h^{p+2}c_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|-2}\|z\|_{s}\|z\|_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|-2}.

Substituting it into (
5.24
), one gets

\displaystyle\|({\bf i}j\partial_{\bar{z}_{j}}W)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\|_{p-1}\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}^{h-2}h^{p+2}\frac{C_{J_{n}}}{e}|||W|||_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}r^{-h}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{\alpha}(\frac{1}{\sigma^{p-1}}|y|^{|\alpha|}\|z\|_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|-1}+\|z\|_{s}\|z\|_{1}^{h-2|\alpha|-2}).

(5.25)

Similar estimate holds for
\|(-{\bf i}j\partial_{\bar{z}_{j}}W)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\|_{p-1}
.

∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Suppose the function

W(x,y,z,\bar{z};\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\mu,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{Z}_{*}}\\
2|\alpha|+|\mu+\gamma|=h\end{subarray}}W^{\alpha\mu\gamma}(x;\xi)y^{\alpha}z^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\gamma}:D(s,r,r)\times\Pi\rightarrow\mathbb{R},

belong to
\mathcal{H}^{*}
. Then, the following estimate hold

\|X_{W}\|_{p,D(s-\sigma,r,r)\times\Pi}\leq c^{h-2}\frac{h^{p+2}}{e\sigma^{p}}|||W|||^{*}_{D(s,r,r)\times\Pi}r^{-2},

where
c
is a constant .

The proof of this Lemma is similar to that of Lemma
5.6
. Here we omit it.

6
Small denominator equation with large variable coefficient

Let
s_{0}>0
be constant and
\epsilon_{0}>0
de small constant. Define sequences
\left\{{{s_{m}}}\right\}_{m=0}^{\infty}
and
\left\{{{\varepsilon_{m}}}\right\}_{m=0}^{\infty}
as follows,

•

{s_{0}}={s_{0}}
,
{s_{m}}={s_{0}}\left({1-\frac{{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}{\frac{1}{{{j^{2}}}}}}}{{100\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}{\frac{1}{{{j^{2}}}}}}}}\right),m=1,2,\cdots
;

•

s_{m}^{(i)}=s_{m+1}+\left(1-\frac{i}{10}\right)(s_{m}-s_{m+1})
,
i=0,1,...,10
;

•

{\varepsilon_{0}}={\varepsilon_{0}}
,
{\varepsilon_{m}}=\varepsilon_{0}^{{{\left({{4\mathord{\left/{\vphantom{43}}\right.\kern-1.2pt}3}}\right)}^{m}}},m=1,2,\cdots
;

Clearly,
{s_{0}}>{s_{1}}>\cdots{s_{m}}>{s_{m+1}}>\cdots>{{{s_{0}}}\mathord{\left/{\vphantom{{{s_{0}}}2}}\right.\kern-1.2pt}2}
and
{s_{m}}>s_{m}^{\left(1\right)}>\cdots s_{m}^{\left(l\right)}>\cdots>{s_{m+1}}
.

Define
D(s)=\left\{{\theta\in{{{{{C^{d}}}\mathord{\left/{\vphantom{{{C^{d}}}{\left({2\pi Z}\right)}}}\right.\kern-1.2pt}{\left({2\pi\mathbb{Z}}\right)}}}^{d}}:\left|{\mathrm{Im}\theta}\right|\leq s}\right\}
, thus

D({s_{0}})\supset D(s_{1})\supset D(s_{2})\supset\cdots\supset D(s_{m})\supset\cdots\supset D(s_{0}/2),

and

D(s_{m})\supset D(s_{m}^{\left(1\right)})\supset D(s_{m}^{\left(2\right)})\supset\cdots\supset D(s_{m}^{\left(l\right)})\supset\cdots\supset D(s_{m+1}).

For any function
f:D(s)\to\mathbb{C}
, we define
{\left\|f\right\|_{s}}=\mathop{\sup}\limits_{\theta\in D(s)}\left|{f\left(\theta\right)}\right|,\forall s\geq 0
. We call that a function
f:D(s)\to\mathbb{C}
is real analytic if it is analytic in
D(s)
and it is real for real variable
\theta\in D(s)
.

Assume that we have a function
a\left(\theta\right):D({s_{m}})\to\mathbb{C}
which obeys

•

a\left(\theta\right)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{m}{{a_{j}}\left(\theta\right)},

(6.1)

•

{a_{j}}\left(\theta\right):D({s_{j}})\to\mathbb{C}
is real analytic, and

\begin{array}[]{l}{\left\|{{a_{j}}}\right\|_{{s_{j}}}}\leq\varepsilon_{j}^{1-}.\end{array}

(6.2)

Let
\omega\in{\left[{0,1}\right]^{d}}
be a parameter vector and
\lambda\neq 0
be a constant which may be large. Assume also that
\left({\omega,\lambda}\right)
is of Diophantine, i.e., there are constants
0<\gamma\ll 1
and
\tau>d
such that

\begin{array}[]{l}\left|{\left\langle{k,\omega}\right\rangle}\right|\geq{{{\gamma_{0}}}\mathord{\left/{\vphantom{{{\gamma_{0}}}{{{\left|k\right|}^{\tau}},\forall k\in{Z^{d}}\backslash\left\{0\right\}}}}\right.\kern-1.2pt}{{{\left|k\right|}^{\tau}},\forall k\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\backslash\left\{0\right\}}},\end{array}

(6.3)

and

\left|{\pm\left\langle{k,\omega}\right\rangle+\lambda}\right|\geq{\gamma_{0}\mathord{\left/{\vphantom{\gamma{{{\left|k\right|}^{\tau}}}}}\right.\kern-1.2pt}{{{\left|k\right|}^{\tau}}}},\forall k\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}.

(6.4)

where we use notation
|k|=1
, when
k=0\in{\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
for convenience.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
A\left(\theta\right):D(s)\to\mathbb{C}
be real analytic for
s>0
. Write
{D_{\omega}}:=\mathbf{i}\,\omega\cdot{\partial_{\theta}}
, where
{\bf i}^{2}=-1
. Consider the differential equation

{D_{\omega}}{\rm X}\left(\theta\right)=A\left(\theta\right).

(6.5)

Then (
6.5
) has unique solution
{\rm X}={\rm X}\left(\theta\right)=D_{\omega}^{-1}A\left(\theta\right):{D(s^{\prime})\to\mathbb{C}}
which is real analytic and

{\left\|{{\rm X}\left(\theta\right)}\right\|_{{s^{\prime}}}}\leq C\left({d,\tau}\right)\frac{1}{{{\gamma_{0}}}}\frac{1}{{{{\left({s-{s^{\prime}}}\right)}^{10\left({d+\tau}\right)}}}}{\left\|A\right\|_{s}},\ \ \forall\ 0<s^{\prime}<s,

(6.6)

where
C\left({d,\tau}\right)
is a constant depending on
d
and
\tau
.

Proof.

The proof is trivial in
KAM
theory. We omit it.
∎

Let

\alpha\left(\theta\right)=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}{{\alpha_{j}}\left(\theta\right)},{\alpha_{j}}\left(\theta\right)=D_{\omega}^{-1}{a_{j}}\left(\theta\right).

(6.7)

By Lemma
6.1
,
{\alpha_{j}}\left(\theta\right):D(s_{j}^{\left(1\right)})\to\mathbb{C}
is real analytic with

{\left\|{{\alpha_{j}}\left(\theta\right)}\right\|_{s_{j}^{\left(1\right)}}}\leq C\left({d,\tau}\right)\frac{1}{{{\gamma_{0}}}}{\left({\frac{1}{{{s_{j}}-s_{j}^{\left(1\right)}}}}\right)^{10\left({d+\tau}\right)}}{\left\|{{a_{j}}}\right\|_{{s_{j}}}}<\varepsilon_{j}^{1-}.

(6.8)

Thus

{\left\|\alpha\right\|_{s_{m}^{\left(1\right)}}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{1-}.

(6.9)

Lemma 6.2
.

Taking any
\theta={\mathop{\rm Re}\nolimits}\ \theta+\mathbf{i}\,{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits}\ \theta\in D(s_{j}^{\left(2\right)})
,we have
\left|{{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits}\ {\alpha_{j}}\left(\theta\right)}\right|<{\varepsilon_{j}^{\frac{1}{{10}}}}\left|{{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits}\ \theta}\right|
.

Proof.

Let
x={\rm Re}\ \theta
,
y={\rm Im}\ \theta
. Then

\displaystyle{\alpha_{j}}\left(\theta\right)

\displaystyle={\alpha_{j}}\left({x+{\bf i}y}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}{\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{d},{|k|=p}}{\frac{{\partial_{x}^{k}{\alpha_{j}}\left(x\right)}}{{k!}}{\bf i}^{p}{y^{k}}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in 2\mathbb{N}}{\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|k|=p}{\frac{{\partial_{x}^{k}{\alpha_{j}}\left(x\right)}}{{k!}}{\bf i}^{p}{y^{k}}}}+\sum_{p\in{2\mathbb{N}+1}}{\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|k|=p}{\frac{{\partial_{x}^{k}{\alpha_{j}}\left(x\right)}}{{k!}}{\bf i}^{p}{y^{k}}}}

Then,

\displaystyle\left|{{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits}\ {\alpha_{j}}\left(\theta\right)}\right|

\displaystyle<\left|{\sum\limits_{p\in 2\mathbb{N}+1}{\left({\pm 1}\right)\sum\limits_{\left|k\right|=p}{\frac{{\partial_{x}^{k}{\alpha_{j}}\left(x\right)}}{{k!}}{y^{k}}}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left({\sum\limits_{p\in 2\mathbb{N}+1}{\left({\sum\limits_{\left|k\right|=p}1}\right){{\left({\frac{1}{{s_{j}^{\left(1\right)}}}}\right)}^{p}}{{\left|y\right|}^{p}}}}\right){\left\|{{\alpha_{j}}}\right\|_{s_{j}^{\left(1\right)}}}

\displaystyle<Cj^{10d^{2}}\frac{1}{{s_{j}^{(1)}}}\varepsilon_{j}^{1-}\left|y\right|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{j}^{\frac{1}{{10}}}\left|{{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits}\ \theta}\right|.

∎

This completes the proof of Lemma
6.2
.

By Lemma
6.2
and noting (
6.7
), we have

\left|{{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits}\ \alpha\left(\theta\right)}\right|\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{{20}}}\left|{{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits}\ \theta}\right|,\quad\forall\ \theta\in D(s_{m}^{(2)}).

(6.10)

Let
s=s_{m},\,s^{\prime}=s_{m}^{(2)}
. Moreover, by (
6.10
), we can define the map
\tilde{T}:D(s^{\prime})\to D(s)
by

\theta=\tilde{T}\varphi=\varphi+\alpha\left(\varphi\right)\omega,\forall\varphi\in\mathbb{T}_{{}_{s^{\prime}}}^{d}.

(6.11)

By (
6.9
), we can prove easily that there is a vector function
\hat{\alpha}:D(s^{\prime\prime})\to D(s^{\prime})
with
s^{\prime\prime}=s_{m}^{(3)}
, which is real analytic such that the map
\tilde{T}
is invertible,
{{\tilde{T}}^{-1}}:D(s^{\prime\prime})\to D(s^{\prime})
is of the form

\varphi={{\tilde{T}}^{-1}}\left(\theta\right)=\theta+\hat{\alpha}\left(\theta\right)\omega,\quad\forall\theta\in D(s^{\prime\prime}),

(6.12)

where
\hat{\alpha}\left(\theta\right)=(\widehat{\alpha}(\theta):j=1,\cdots,d)\in{\mathbb{C}^{d}}
,
\hat{\alpha}\left(\theta\right)\omega=(\alpha_{j}(\theta)\omega_{j}:j=1,\cdots,d)
, and

{\left|\left|{\hat{\alpha}}\right|\right|_{s^{\prime\prime}}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{1-},\quad s^{\prime\prime}=s_{m}^{\left(3\right)}.

(6.13)

Actually, the proof is as follows. Let
\varphi={\tilde{T}^{-1}}\theta=\theta+h\left(\theta\right)
. Since
\theta=\varphi+\alpha\left(\varphi\right)\omega
, we have
\theta=\theta+h\left(\theta\right)+\alpha\left({\theta+h\left(\theta\right)}\right)\omega
, by which we get a Picard sequence

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{h_{0}}\left(\theta\right)=-\alpha\left(\theta\right)\omega,\\
{h_{\nu}}\left(\theta\right)=-\alpha\left({\theta+{h_{\nu-1}}\left(\theta\right)}\right)\omega,\quad\nu=1,2,\cdots.\end{array}\right.

(6.14)

By contraction mapping principle, as well as
(
6.9
)
and
(
6.10
)
, there exists

h\left(\theta\right)=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{\nu\to\infty}{h_{\nu}}\left(\theta\right):D(s^{\prime\prime})\to D(s^{\prime})

which is real analytic. Let
\hat{\alpha}\left(\theta\right)=-\alpha\left({\theta+h\left(\theta\right)}\right)
, we have

{\left\|{\hat{\alpha}}\right\|_{s^{\prime\prime}}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{1-},\quad\left|{{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits}\ \hat{\alpha}\left(\theta\right)}\right|\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{1-}\left|{{\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits}\ \theta}\right|,\quad\forall\theta\in D(s^{\prime\prime}).

(6.15)

By
{{\tilde{T}}^{-1}}
, we also induce a linear operator
{{\tilde{T}}^{-1}}:\mathcal{L}\left(D(s^{\prime\prime})\right)\to\mathcal{L}\left(D(s^{\prime})\right)
by

\left({{{\tilde{T}}^{-1}}f}\right)\left(\theta\right)=f\left({{{\tilde{T}}^{-1}}\theta}\right)=f\left({\theta+\hat{\alpha}\left(\theta\right)\omega}\right)=f\left(\varphi\right),

(6.16)

where
\mathcal{L}\left(D(s)\right)
denotes the set of all real analytic functions defined in
D(s)
.

Lemma 6.3
.

The homological equation

\left({i\omega\cdot{\partial_{\varphi}}+\lambda\left({1+a\left(\varphi\right)}\right)}\right)x\left(\varphi\right)=R\left(\varphi\right),\varphi\in D(s_{m})

(6.17)

has a unique solution
x=x(\varphi):D(s_{m+1})\to\mathbb{C}
which is real analytic and

\|x\|_{s_{m+1}}\leq C(d,\tau)\frac{1}{\gamma}(\frac{1}{s_{m}-s_{m+1}})^{20(d+\tau)}\|R\|_{s_{m}},

(6.18)

where
(\omega,\lambda)
satisfies the Diophantine (
6.3
), (
6.4
) and
a=a(\theta)
obeys (
6.1
),(
6.2
), and
R=R{\varphi}:D(s_{m})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is real analytic.

Proof.

Let
x=x(\varphi)
be the solution of the homological equation (
6.17
).
And let
\tilde{T}^{-1}x=y
. Then
x(\varphi)=(\tilde{T}y)(\varphi)
where
y=y(\theta):D(s^{\prime\prime})\to D(s^{\prime})
. Then

x\left(\varphi\right)=y\left({\tilde{T}\varphi}\right)=y\left(\theta\right)=y\left({\varphi+\alpha\left(\varphi\right)\omega}\right).

(6.19)

By computation, we have

\displaystyle\omega\cdot{\partial_{\varphi}}x\left(\varphi\right)

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot{\partial_{\varphi}}y\left({\varphi+\alpha\left(\varphi\right)\omega}\right)

(6.20)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}{{\omega_{j}}{\partial_{{\varphi_{1}}}}y\left({{\varphi_{j}}+\alpha\left(\varphi\right){\omega_{1}},\cdots,{\varphi_{d}}+\alpha\left(\varphi\right){\omega_{d}}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}{{\omega_{j}}{\partial_{{\theta_{j}}}}y\left(\theta\right)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d}{\sum\limits_{j=1}^{d}{{\omega_{i}}{\omega_{j}}{\partial_{{\theta_{i}}}}y\left(\theta\right)}}{\partial_{{\varphi_{j}}}}\alpha\left(\varphi\right)

\displaystyle=(\omega\cdot{\partial_{\theta}}y\left(\theta\right))\left({1+\omega\cdot{\partial_{\varphi}}\alpha\left(\varphi\right)}\right),

where
\theta=\tilde{T}\varphi=\varphi+\alpha\left(\varphi\right)\omega
. And

\lambda\left({1+a\left(\varphi\right)}\right)x\left(\varphi\right)=\lambda\left({1+a\left(\varphi\right)}\right)y\left(\theta\right)=\lambda\left({1+a\left({{{\tilde{T}}^{-1}}\theta}\right)}\right)y\left(\theta\right).

(6.21)

By (
6.20
) and (
6.21
), the homological equation (
6.17
) can be rewritten as

\left({\bf i}\omega\cdot{\partial_{\theta}}y\left(\theta\right)\right)\left({1+\omega\cdot{\partial_{\varphi}}\alpha\left(\varphi\right)}\right)+\lambda\left({1+a\left(\varphi\right)}\right)y\left(\theta\right)=R\left(\varphi\right).

(6.22)

By (
6.7
),
D_{\omega}^{-1}a\left(\theta\right)=\alpha\left(\theta\right),\forall\theta\in D(s^{\prime\prime})
, that implies
\omega\cdot{\partial_{\varphi}}\alpha\left(\varphi\right)=a\left(\varphi\right)
.
Let
\tilde{R}\left(\varphi\right)={\left({1+a\left(\varphi\right)}\right)^{-1}}R\left(\varphi\right)
. Then (
6.22
) reads

{\bf i}\,\omega\cdot{\partial_{\theta}}y\left(\theta\right)+\lambda y\left(\theta\right)=\tilde{R}\left(\varphi\right)=\tilde{R}\left({{{\tilde{T}}^{-1}}\theta}\right):={R^{*}}\left(\theta\right),

(6.23)

where we take
\theta\in D(s^{\prime\prime})
. By (
6.2
),

{\left\|{\tilde{R}}\right\|_{s^{\prime\prime}}}\leq{\left({1+c{\varepsilon_{0}}}\right)^{-1}}{\left\|R\right\|_{{s_{m}}}}\leq 2{\left\|R\right\|_{{s_{m}}}}.

(6.24)

Moreover, by (
6.15
),

{\left\|{\tilde{R}\left({{{\tilde{T}}^{-1}}\left(\theta\right)}\right)}\right\|_{s^{\prime\prime}}}\leq 2{\left\|R\right\|_{{s_{m}}}}.

(6.25)

By Fourier expansion, the solution to (
6.23
) is

y\left(\theta\right)=\sum\limits_{k\in{Z^{d}}}{\frac{1}{{\lambda-\left\langle{k,\omega}\right\rangle}}}{{\hat{R}}^{*}}\left(k\right){e^{ik\theta}},\theta\in D(s^{\prime\prime}),

(6.26)

where
\widehat{R}^{*}(k)
is k-Forier coefficient of
R^{*}
.
By the Diophantine Conditions (
6.3
),

\displaystyle{\left\|y\right\|_{s_{m}^{(3)}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left({d,\tau}\right)\frac{1}{\gamma}\frac{1}{{{{\left({s^{\prime\prime}-s_{m}^{(3)}}\right)}^{10\left({d+\tau}\right)}}}}{\left\|{{R^{*}}}\right\|_{s^{\prime\prime}}}

(6.27)

\displaystyle\leq C\left({d,\tau}\right)\frac{1}{\gamma}\frac{1}{{{{\left({s^{\prime\prime}-s_{m}^{(3)}}\right)}^{10\left({d+\tau}\right)}}}}{\left\|{{R^{*}}}\right\|_{{s_{m}}}},

Returning to (
6.19
), we have

{\left\|{x\left(\varphi\right)}\right\|_{s_{m+1}}}\leq C\left({d,\tau}\right)\frac{1}{\gamma}\frac{1}{{{{\left({s_{m}-s_{m+1}}\right)}^{10\left({d+\tau}\right)}}}}{\left\|{{R^{*}}}\right\|_{{s_{m}}}}.

(6.28)

This completes the proof.
∎

7
